Append-only log of everything the agents say to each other and to the orchestrators.
Format: `### <UTC time> · <agent>` then the message. Agents read this file to answer questions
addressed to them (`@<agent>:`). Written through `scripts/swarm-say.sh`.

@M4: M5 wiring for main.ts (all APIs exist and are tested; types: Menu/GamepadInput are in src/ui/Menu.ts and src/core/Gamepad.ts).
1) imports:
   import { Menu } from '@/ui/Menu';
   import { GamepadInput } from '@/core/Gamepad';
2) right after `if (isTouch) new TouchControls(overlay, input);` (needs input+hud+audio+pipeline+renderer; `game` is used lazily inside callbacks so it may be declared later, but use `let`-free arrow bodies as below):
   const gamepad = new GamepadInput(input, { onToast: (m) => hud.showToast(m), onStart: () => menu.toggle(), onNav: (d) => menu.navigate(d) });
   const menu = new Menu(overlay, {
     onPause: (p) => { game.paused = p || document.hidden; gamepad.paused = p; audio.setPaused(p); if (!p) { input.endFrame(); input.requestLock(); } },
     onQuality: (q) => { quality = q; pipeline.setQuality(q); setupShadows(renderer, dayNight.sun, q); },
     onMasterVolume: (v) => audio.setMasterVolume(v),
     onRadioVolume: (v) => audio.setRadioVolume(v),
     onInvertLookY: (b) => { input.invertLookY = b; },
     onReset: () => { const b = player.vehicle ? player.vehicle.body : player.body; b.pos.x = 9; b.pos.z = 20; b.vel.x = 0; b.vel.z = 0; },
   }, { touch: isTouch, initialQuality: quality, ignoreSavedQuality: params.has('q') });
   input.onModeChange = (m) => { hud.setInputMode(m); menu.setInputMode(m); };
   hud.setInputMode(input.mode); menu.setInputMode(input.mode);
   (Menu fires onMasterVolume/onRadioVolume/onInvertLookY/onQuality once at construction for any persisted values; `game`, `audio`, `pipeline` must exist by then, so construct Menu AFTER `const game = new Game(...)`, or move `gamepad`+`menu` just below it.) Reset spawn is (9,20) as in `new Player('player', {x:9,z:20})`.
3) visibilitychange line: replace with `document.addEventListener('visibilitychange', () => { game.paused = document.hidden || menu.isOpen; });` (AudioSystem already self-handles hidden tab; do NOT also call audio.pause for it.)
4) per frame in the Game render callback (runs even while paused, which Start/menu nav need): 
   const nowPad = performance.now(); gamepad.inVehicle = player.inVehicle; gamepad.update(Math.min(0.1, (nowPad - lastPad) / 1000)); lastPad = nowPad;   // declare `let lastPad = performance.now();` above
5) minimap: pass dt as 4th arg if you have it (optional): minimap.update(camTarget, camHeading, copPositions, frameDt). If omitted it self-times.
6) audio, next to audio.setEngine(...): 
   const pv = player.vehicle as ({ slip?: number } & typeof player.vehicle) | null;
   audio.setSlip(pv?.slip ?? 0); audio.setHandbrake(player.inVehicle && player.lastVehicleInput.handbrake);   // M2 may rename slip, see transcript
7) prompt: keep sending `${key}  Enter car` with key 'F'; Hud rewrites the glyph (F/CAR/A) per input mode itself, so `const key = isTouch ? 'CAR' : 'F'` can stay or become 'F'.
8) Hud also has hud.showToast(msg), hud.showLocationCard(title, sub) (auto "Welcome to Bed-Stuy" on first hideSplash). Esc/pointer-lock-loss opens the menu by itself.

D1 starting: schema v2 for lots. Files: src/world/data/schema.ts (types+validator+loadFaces+lotsForFace), src/world/data/bedstuy.json (+ optional top-level 'faces'), tests/unit/world/data-schema.test.ts. No Grid.ts change planned.
@B3 @S4: D1 API (code against this now; module is src/world/data/schema.ts, landing within ~30 min):
  import { loadFaces, lotsForFace, faceFor, STOREFRONT_TO_ARCHETYPE } from '@/world/data/schema';
  import type { FaceSet, FaceDef, LotSpec, PlacedLot, Family, Cornice, LotUse, StorefrontKind, Confidence } from '@/world/data/schema';
  const faces: FaceSet = loadFaces(bedstuyJson);          // validates (throws SchemaError listing every problem), applies defaults; json without 'faces' -> empty set
  const def: FaceDef | undefined = faceFor(faces, blockFace);  // matches BlockFace by (streetId, side, from/to cross streets)
  const lots: PlacedLot[] = lotsForFace(grid, blockFace, faces, opts?);   // ALWAYS returns lots (data if present, else typology default generated from grid.seed + face key), ordered along face.a -> face.b (same as Lot.frontA->frontB), widths scaled so sum == |a-b| (or minus opts.insetA/insetB).
  LotSpec (all resolved, no undefined): { widthM, floors, family: 'brownstone'|'brickRed'|'brickTan'|'brickPainted'|'limestone'|'frame'|'church'|'vacant'|'newBuild', stoop: boolean, stoopHeightM, cornice: 'bracketed'|'plain'|'none', bays, use: 'residential'|'mixed'|'church'|'school'|'vacant'|'garage', storefront?: 'bodega'|'laundromat'|'barber'|'deli'|'liquor'|'pharmacy'|'church'|'daycare'|'restaurant'|'cellphone'|'generic' (set iff use==='mixed' or church/school storefront), trees, scaffold, confidence: 'H'|'M'|'L', note }
  PlacedLot = LotSpec & { id (= `${blockId}-${side}-${i}`, same scheme as LotSubdivider), index, offsetM (from face.a), frontA, frontB (Vec2), center, outward, depthM, corner (first/last lot), fromData }
  STOREFRONT_TO_ARCHETYPE: Record<StorefrontKind, types.Archetype> (barber->barbershop, deli->bodega, restaurant->caribbean, cellphone->dollar, generic->dollar).
Data keys in JSON: faces[] = { street, side:'N'|'S'|'E'|'W' (block side, as BlockFace.side), from, to (cross-street ids, from = west/north end), lots?: [...authoring order from->to...], typology?, pins? }. Questions/changes welcome via @D1.

### 2026-10-28T22:01:22Z · X5
@P8: mission wiring request for src/main.ts (all new files exist and typecheck; tests green).
IMPORTS:
import { MissionSystem } from '@/game/missions/MissionSystem';
import { buildMissions } from '@/game/missions/content';
import { MissionMarkerView } from '@/game/missions/MissionMarkerView';
import { saveMissionData, loadMissionData } from '@/game/missions/MissionStore';
import { MissionHud } from '@/ui/MissionHud';
CONSTRUCT (after `wanted` is created; world.lots must be the built lots):
const missions = new MissionSystem(buildMissions(grid, data, world.lots), {
  persist: saveMissionData,
  raiseWanted: (stars, pos) => { let g = 3; while (wanted.stars < stars && g-- > 0) events.emit('crime', { type: 'carjack', pos }); },
});
missions.restore(loadMissionData());
const missionMarkers = new MissionMarkerView();
scene.add(missionMarkers.group);
UI (after `const hud = new Hud(overlay)`):
const missionHud = new MissionHud(overlay);
SIM STEP (in the 'simulation' system, right after wanted.update(dt)):
{ const pv = player.vehicle; const b = (pv ?? player).body;
  missions.update(dt, b.pos, player.inVehicle, wanted.stars, pv ? Math.abs(pv.speed) : Math.hypot(b.vel.x, b.vel.z), dayNight.timeOfDay);
  if (input.pressed('KeyR')) missions.retry(dayNight.timeOfDay);
  if (input.pressed('KeyX')) missions.abandon(); }
RENDER CALLBACK (next to hud.update(hudState)):
missionHud.update(missions.hud, frameDt);
missionMarkers.sync(missions.markers, missions.markersVersion); missionMarkers.update(performance.now() / 1000);
Notes: R/X are unused elsewhere (retry prompt text is on the HUD; pass {retryKey,abandonKey} in options if you map a gamepad/touch button, call missions.retry()/abandon() from it). Missions start by walking into the gold giver circle; deadline/time windows use dayNight.timeOfDay (Block Party is offered 6am-6pm, due 20:00; default start 17:30 gives 150 s). Optional debug hook: missions.start('bodega-run').

@B3 @S4:
  import { getBedstuyFaces, faceSpecResolver, allLots } from '@/world/data/faces';   // validated real data, cached
  import { lotsForFace, lotsForBlock, faceFor, loadFaces } from '@/world/data/schema;
  - faceFor(set, grid, blockFace) (grid is a required 2nd arg), lotsForFace(grid, blockFace, set, opts?) -> PlacedLot[]; lotsForBlock(grid, blockId, set).
  - faceSpecResolver(grid, set?) returns (blockId, side) => PlacedLot[] | null: drop-in for B3's subdivideLots 4th arg. Rows are ordered along face.a -> face.b, widths sum to |a-b| minus the corner insets, ids are `${blockId}-${side}-${i}` (same as LotSubdivider).
  - N/S faces are inset automatically by the neighbouring E/W corner lots' depthM (E/W faces include their two corner lots). So do NOT add your own corner inset on top when using these rows.
  - PlacedLot = LotSpec + { id, blockId, side, index, offsetM, frontA, frontB, center, outward, corner, cornerEnd?: 'a'|'b', cornerStreetId?: string (the cross street at that corner, S4's question), fromData }.
  - LotSpec fields: widthM, floors (TOTAL storeys incl. ground floor; mixed lot = 1 shop + floors-1 above; S4's 'floors above' = floors-1; 0 only for vacant), family, stoop (bool) + stoopHeightM, cornice, bays, use, storefront?, trees (0..3), scaffold, depthM, confidence, note, sign?, awningColor? STOREFRONT_TO_ARCHETYPE maps to types.Archetype. No cuisine/name/closesAt in the schema; S4 derives those (restaurant -> cuisine by rng).

### 2026-10-28T22:07:33Z · H7
FOR FABLE: vite.config.ts + package.json + .gitignore for the hosted build (tested via a scratch copy of the repo: artifact build unchanged 6.70 MB incl. new code, hosted build 37 files 6.0 MB with assets/three-<hash>.js split, textures as hashed files, 0 base64 textures, headless smoke ?q=low and ?q=ultra: 0 console errors, 0 failed requests).
(1) vite.config.ts, replace the whole file with this (full text also at /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-GTA-Bedstuy/b724b335-0535-5878-9045-0474ae836c7e/scratchpad/vite.config.proposed.ts; patch at .../scratchpad/vite.config.patch):
--- begin ---
import { defineConfig } from 'vite';
import { viteSingleFile } from 'vite-plugin-singlefile';
import path from 'node:path';

/**
 * Two builds from one codebase (docs/swarm/PRD.md section 2.3):
 *  - default (`npm run build`, `npm run artifact`): everything inlined into dist/index.html (single-file artifact).
 *  - hosted  (`VITE_HOSTED=1`, `npm run build:hosted`): normal multi-file output with hashed assets, three.js in its own
 *    chunk, textures emitted as files (served and cached by the host) instead of base64 data URIs.
 */
const hosted = process.env.VITE_HOSTED === '1';

export default defineConfig({
  base: './',
  resolve: { alias: { '@': path.resolve(import.meta.dirname, 'src') } },
  plugins: hosted ? [] : [viteSingleFile()],
  build: hosted
    ? {
        target: 'es2022',
        assetsInlineLimit: 4096,
        cssCodeSplit: true,
        reportCompressedSize: false,
        chunkSizeWarningLimit: 1500,
        rolldownOptions: {
          output: {
            codeSplitting: { groups: [{ name: 'three', test: /node_modules[\\/]three[\\/]/, priority: 10 }] },
            entryFileNames: 'assets/[name]-[hash].js',
            chunkFileNames: 'assets/[name]-[hash].js',
            assetFileNames: 'assets/[name]-[hash][extname]',
          },
        },
      }
    : { target: 'es2022', assetsInlineLimit: 100_000_000, cssCodeSplit: false, reportCompressedSize: false },
  test: {
    include: ['tests/unit/**/*.test.ts'],
    environment: 'node',
    testTimeout: 30_000,
  },
});
--- end ---
(2) package.json scripts, add after the "artifact" line (and put a comma on it):
    "build:hosted": "node scripts/build-hosted.mjs --only hosted",
    "build:all": "node scripts/build-hosted.mjs",
    "smoke:hosted": "node scripts/build-hosted.mjs --only hosted --smoke --port 4307"
   Optional: "engines": { "node": ">=20.19" } (Vite 8 floor; Vercel picks a compatible Node).
(3) .gitignore: add a line  dist-hosted
Notes: default 'npm run build' / 'npm run artifact' are byte-for-byte the old behaviour (no VITE_HOSTED = singlefile on). vercel.json (landed) runs 'VITE_HOSTED=1 npm run build' with output 'dist', so the Vercel dashboard needs only the Vite preset; no dashboard env var needed.

It also fixes today's crash path where an unknown ?q= value is cast straight into Quality.

### 2026-10-28T22:09:51Z · B3
B3 progress (landed in tree, tsc + world tests green):
- API I implemented against D1's schema.ts: subdivideLots(grid, pois, rng, faces?: FaceSet). 3-arg call is byte-identical to before (no spec). With a FaceSet, every face for which faceFor(...) exists is cut by lotsForFace (ids, fronts, depthM, corner) and each Lot carries .spec (builder-facing LotSpec from specFromData(PlacedLot)); faces without a FaceDef keep the procedural subdivision (same rng stream); N/S faces get the inset of whichever E/W corner depths were used. Not using faceSpecResolver - it takes the FaceSet directly. @P8 WIRING (one line in WorldBuilder.buildWorld): 'import { getBedstuyFaces } from "@/world/data/faces"; const lots = subdivideLots(grid, data.pois, rng.fork("lots"), getBedstuyFaces());' buildBuildings(lots, materials, rng) unchanged (reads lot.spec when present; type SpecLot exported from BuildingGen). Needs no types.ts change.
- floors convention = D1's: storeys above the raised basement incl. the parlour (brownstone 3 = parlour + 2). Heights: stoop/basement stoopHeightM, parlour 3.3, upper 2.9 (facade.ts PARLOUR_H/UPPER_H/storeyLevels; FLOOR_H kept = 3.3 for S4).
- @S4 HAND-OFF: I added to facade.ts  buildUpperFloors(c: LotCtx, o: { y0: number; floors: number; wall: Material; trim?: Material; cornice?: 'none'|'plain'|'dentil'|'bracket'; bays?: number; fireEscape?: boolean; z?: number }): { topY: number; sillYs: number[] }.  It draws EVERYTHING from y0 up: party-wall shell box (y0..topY, corner side left open), front wall with recessed double-hung windows (hood lintel on first floor, sills, top frame), string courses, bracketed/dentil cornice, parapet+roof+clutter, tank, fire escape or AC units, drain pipe and the windowed corner side wall above y0. You keep 0..y0 (front wall below y0 incl. piers/glazing, the side wall below y0 for corner lots, shell box 0..y0). floors = storeys ABOVE the shop (= spec.floors - 1 for mixed lots); each is 2.9 m (UPPER_H). Typical call: const { topY } = buildUpperFloors(c, { y0: 4.0, floors: (c.spec?.floors ?? 3) - 1, wall: brick, trim: m.limestone }). Per-lot data reaches you as c.spec (LotCtx.spec?: LotSpec from facade.ts): { floors, widthM, family: brownstone|brick|tanBrick|paintedBrick|limestone, stoop:{present,heightM}, cornice, bays?, use: residential|apartment|mixed|church|vacant|institutional, storefront?: string (D1 StorefrontKind), trees, scaffold?, arched? }; the POI is still lot.poi (and Lot.spec.use === 'mixed' when the data or a POI made it a shop). ### 2026-10-28T22:17:55Z · Q10
FOR FABLE (src/entities/** and src/game/Traffic* have no Stage 2 owner): DEFECT Q10-D1 AI cars rear-end and T-bone each other — ~30 AI-vs-AI impacts with closing speed >= 3 m/s per 5 simulated minutes (seed 7: 121 veh-veh impacts, up to 13.7 m/s at intersections; seed 21: 102). Repro: tests/unit/sim/traffic-agent-qa.test.ts 'Q10-D1' (stalled car C in the lane, two followers queue behind it, after ~7 s the pass clips C at 6.1 m/s) and the integrated tests/unit/sim/traffic-5min.test.ts. Root cause (traced frame by frame in a seed-21 rear-end at (-84,0)): TrafficAgent.update casts its obstacle ray from rayFrom = pos - right*this.lat, i.e. from the intended lane-offset line, while the body has not moved there yet. When a pass starts (lat eases up at 2.5 m/s) or is cancelled (passBody dropped because the blocker moves > 1.5 m/s) the ray misses the car ahead and the agent accelerates (throttle 1.0) into it at 8 m/s. Two stopped queue cars also begin passing each other simultaneously. Proposed fix: always cast the forward ray from the real body position (lat = 0) and, when lat != 0, a second ray from the offset line; take the nearer hit; also forbid starting a pass while the blocker is itself a traffic car with passBody set or moving > 0.3 m/s. Expected after fix: <= 2 such impacts per 5 min (test flips from it.fails to it).

### 2026-10-28T22:17:56Z · Q10
FOR FABLE: DEFECT Q10-D3 AI cars knock pedestrians down on WALK — 3 knock-downs in seed 7 (t=178 s and 186 s ped9 at Throop x Lafayette, t=269 s ped36 at Throop x Kosciuszko) and 1 in seed 21, all while the ped is mid-crosswalk with walkState 'walk', hit by a TURNING car (heading 1.0-2.2 rad, 1-5 m/s). Repro: 'Q10-D3' it.fails in tests/unit/sim/traffic-5min.test.ts (knockedDown must be empty for seeds 7 and 21). Cause: TrafficAgent.scanPeds only looks along the current heading (lateral <= 2.4 m, along 0.5..reach), so a car that is mid-turn does not see the ped in the crosswalk it is turning into. Proposed fix: when the target/next node is within ~12 m and the heading error to it exceeds ~0.3 rad, scan the cone toward the target node (or the crosswalk rect of the intersection leg it will exit on) instead of straight ahead; vehicles turning across a crosswalk with a ped on it hold at the stop line.

### 2026-10-28T22:17:56Z · Q10
FOR FABLE: DEFECT Q10-D2 'impact' event spam — Vehicle.onContact fires an impact event (crash sound, camera shake, hitCop check) on EVERY frame while a pedestrian or scraping car stays pressed against a car: the scrape filter only drops repeats with impulse <= lastHitImpulse, so a slowly rising closing speed (3.00 -> 3.20) defeats it. Seen in the 5-min sim: 544 events from one ped against a parked car, 99 from one fleeing ped, in 5 minutes. Repro: tests/unit/sim/traffic-agent-qa.test.ts 'Q10-D2' (60 contacts in 1 s emit 60 events, expected <= 2). Proposed fix in Vehicle.onContact: treat a repeat within SCRAPE_WINDOW as a scrape unless contact.impulse > lastHitImpulse * 1.5 + 1 (genuinely harder hit), and refresh lastHitTime on every contact so a continuous press never leaves the window.

### 2026-10-28T22:17:56Z · Q10
FOR FABLE: DEFECT Q10-D4 (low) TrafficAgent wedges forever on a signal pole when off-lane — a car pushed off its lane steers straight at its target node; a 0.12 m signal pole on that line stops it, the 8 s stuck detector backs it 2 m and it returns to the identical line (loops for the whole 60 s). Seen in a cop pile-up at the map edge (garvey x hart, signal:garvey|hart:1:1). Repro: 'Q10-D4' in tests/unit/sim/traffic-agent-qa.test.ts. Proposed fix: after the 2nd back-off retry (stuckRetries >= 2) call resync() to the nearest forward lane node, or back up with a random steering lock so the retry takes a different line.

### 2026-11-01T15:20:13Z · V1
REPRO CONFIRMED in real build (headless, F via page.keyboard, swiftshader ~10 fps): F enters fine, second F exits then RE-ENTERS the same car (inVehicle false but isEntering true, then true again). ROOT CAUSE: Input.pressed('KeyF') stays true for every fixed step of a frame (Game.endFrame clears the edge once per FRAME). On any frame running >=2 steps (all slow machines; this game is heavy) the sim exits on step 1 and tryEnter()s the same car (within 3 m) on step 2. Same double-fire hits E radio. FIX: new Input.consume(code) (true once per press, survives zero-step frames), used for F in main.ts:416 and E in Player.control. Unit test player-edge.test.ts (11) incl. guard proving old pressed() re-enters. FOR FABLE: main.ts:425-426 replace input.pressed('KeyR')/('KeyX') with input.consume('KeyR')/('KeyX') (same double-fire: retry/abandon twice per slow frame). Now writing e2e enter-exit.mjs.

### 2026-11-01T15:22:08Z · MGR
MGR: ROUNDTABLE OPEN. The user wants to hear us think out loud, so this is an open discussion running alongside the work. Every running lane (V1, PT, L10, W2, MOB, MDL, RADIO, BIBLE) please answer all six questions below in one post headed 'ROUNDTABLE <your id>', three to six plain-English sentences per question, with your reasoning, and react to at least one other lane's answer by name (a second, shorter post is fine for the reactions once others have spoken). Answer from what you actually know from your own work today, and say so when you are guessing. I will read everything, nudge anyone who has not answered in fifteen minutes, and then post a synthesis with the decisions I draw from it.

Q1. What would make someone want to live in this Bed-Stuy for a whole day, not just drive through it for five minutes? Think about what they would see, hear and do between 7 in the morning and 2 at night.
Q2. What does GTA 5 do with the camera that we do not do yet? Be concrete: on foot, in a car, during a chase, in a cutscene or mission start.
Q3. Is the phone tier a different game or the same game made smaller? What must survive on the phone for it to still feel like this place, and what can honestly go?
Q4. How should the radio feel when the user's own songs come on? Think about the moment of switching stations, the jingles between songs, the talk station, and what happens when a slot is empty.
Q5. What makes a mission funny here? The tone is neighbourhood parody, warm rather than cruel; what kind of situation, dialogue or failure would make a player laugh?
Q6. What did your lane find hardest today, and why? Include anything that surprised you or that you would warn the next person about.

### 2026-11-01T15:44:28Z · MDLI
MDLI decision: the Kenney cars are toy-proportioned (1.5 m wide and 1.3 m tall for 2.55 m long) and they share one palette texture for the whole car, so I changed two things in the loader. First, a glTF car is now fitted to the vehicle spec box per axis (length, width, height) while the wheels keep a uniform scale, so the cars are not 2.7 m wide and the wheels stay round and on the ground. Second, the body colour is recoloured only inside the paint cell of the palette atlas by a small PaletteCarPaint material, so windows, tyres and trim keep their colours and the existing colour variants still work. Headlights, taillights, plates and the cop light bar are synthesised on the body surface with rays, so night lights and sirens keep working. For far cars, CarLod now builds one merged, instance-coloured model per glTF type, so draw calls should not jump.

### 2026-11-01T15:45:29Z · MGR
MGR correction: I was wrong to call V1 quiet; V1 posted a confirmed root cause at 15:20 and I missed it while reading. In plain words: the game runs its simulation in fixed 1/60 s steps, and on a slow machine one drawn frame can contain two or more of those steps. The F key's 'just pressed' flag was only cleared once per drawn frame, so the first step got you out of the car and the second step saw F still 'just pressed' and put you straight back in, because you were standing right next to the door. That explains why the user, on a heavy scene, could never get out, and why a fast desktop might not show it. V1's fix is a consume() call that hands out each key press exactly once, which is the right shape of fix rather than a timing hack. FOR FABLE (V1 15:20): replace input.pressed('KeyR') and input.pressed('KeyX') in main.ts (mission retry and abandon, around lines 425-426) with input.consume('KeyR') and input.consume('KeyX'). MGR recommends APPROVE: the same double-fire would retry or abandon a mission twice on a slow frame, it is a two-word change per line, and the gate afterwards is tsc plus tests/unit/game. I would also ask V1 to list every other pressed() call in main.ts and Player in its report (radio E is already covered), so we know none is left.

### 2026-11-01T16:01:50Z · PT
ROUNDTABLE PT. Q1 (living here for a day): I only know this from building a way to watch a stranger play, so this is partly a guess. A day is made of small repeatable moments: the corner bodega rolling its gate up at 6, school-hour foot traffic, a stoop conversation, the lights coming on at dusk, the gates going down at 22:00. What I can offer is a way to find out: the blind testers will tell us in their own words, with their thoughts burned into the video, which of those moments they noticed and which they walked straight past. Q2 (camera vs GTA 5): from the harness side the thing I notice is that on foot our camera only moves when the player moves the mouse, while a good third-person camera also helps: it eases behind you when you run, swings wide on a sprint, and frames the car in a chase. In my own scripted drive the camera slowly recentres behind the car, which is right, but a tester using a look command cannot easily look at a pursuer. I am guessing here, I have not compared frame by frame. Q3 (phone tier): I would say the same game made smaller, because a tester can describe the place only through what they see on the street: brownstones, shop signs, the light, the sound. Those must survive; shadows quality, post effects and view distance can go. MOB's tier work matters here, and my harness takes the quality tier as a flag so a tester can play the phone tier and report whether it still reads as Bed-Stuy. Q4 (the radio): I have not played it, so I will only say what a recorded session would show: the moment of switching stations is the part a viewer will see (the prompt and the station name), so a short, readable station card matters more than anything technical. An empty slot should say so plainly instead of going silent without explanation. I am reacting to RADIO's work here, not testing it. Q5 (funny missions): the funniest thing I can see from a tester's point of view is the gap between a very serious objective line and an ordinary street, for example a timer ticking down for something small. Failure can be funny too if the retry line has a personality. A tester reading mission text aloud as a subtitle will tell us at once if a line lands. Q6 (hardest today): the hardest part was not the code but the machine: software rendering is slow, several lanes ran headless browsers at once, and my first three validation runs were killed by out-of-memory or screenshot timeouts. The fix was to stop drawing frames while nobody needs them (the world is frozen while the tester thinks and while a screenshot is taken) and to count game time in simulation steps instead of seconds. I also react to V1: the root cause V1 found, a just-pressed flag cleared once per drawn frame while several simulation steps ran, is exactly the kind of bug that only shows up on a slow renderer, which is what the testers will run on. Warning for the next person: always use the render lock and a free port.

### 2026-11-01T16:12:56Z · MOB
ROUNDTABLE MOB. Q1 (living here for a day): I only see the street through the draw-call counter, so this is partly a guess, but the places that read as lived-in in my captures are the repeated small things: tree canopies leaning over the sidewalk, shop awnings, stoops, lit windows at dusk, signals changing. Those are exactly what is cheapest to keep, so I would build the day around them rather than around big set pieces. Q2 (GTA 5 camera): I have not compared frame by frame, but one thing I do know from the phone work is that a camera that sits close to the player hides the view distance limit. On the phone tier the street ends in fog at about 110 m, and a low chase camera makes that feel like atmosphere instead of a limit. Q3 (phone tier): my answer is the same game made smaller, and I measured what that costs. What survives: the street layout, brick and stone facades, stoops and cornices, awnings and signs, trees, parked and moving cars, the time-of-day colour and the night lamps. What honestly goes: shadows, ambient occlusion, depth of field, bloom, fire escapes and rooftop tanks, wires, trash bags, iron fences, manholes, and two thirds of the crowd. On three of my captures the street still reads as Bed-Stuy, and the cut is closer to a lower-detail version of the same block than to a different game; the one thing I would warn about is that without shadows the sun direction is harder to feel, so the sky and fog colour must carry the time of day. Q4 (radio): not my lane and I have not played it; my only input is that on a phone the audio must not compete for the main thread, so decoding should happen when a station is chosen, not per frame. Q5 (funny missions): guessing, but the funniest failures in games are small and physical, so a mission where the bodega cat or a double-parked box truck blocks the getaway costs us nothing in rendering. Q6 (hardest today): measuring. The software renderer on this box is shared by many lanes, so one capture took many minutes and pages crashed from memory pressure; I learned to measure draw calls and triangles instead of frame time, because those do not depend on the machine's load. Reaction: I agree with PT that a blind tester playing the phone tier is the right test of Q3, and I will make sure the harness flag ?q=phone works and that the fog does not eat the shop signs.

GTA 5 keeps the camera behind the player, swings and widens it with speed, tucks it close when you are on foot, and has a different framing on a bike. Ours looks fixed. In my curb screenshots the street-level view was nice at eye height, so a lower, closer view on foot might help sell the world.

The humour comes from how specific and local the places feel, not from big jokes.

### 2026-11-01T16:13:40Z · BIBLE
ROUNDTABLE BIBLE
Q1 Live here for a day: The place has to keep moving without you and always offer something one choice away. That means a stranger, an event or a gag within about 30 seconds of walking, and an hour-by-hour day: school run, noon stoops, golden hour, block party, quiet night cops. People stay when the street has moods and somebody is always talking to them.
Q2 Camera: GTA gives the player a look-back, a separate cinematic camera, hood and first-person views, and letterboxed mission intros. Its follow camera is also said to lag and lean in corners, though the public sources only describe that through mod changelogs. We have a solid follow camera with shake and wall pull-in but none of those extras, and the camera yaw does not swing out in a turn.
Q3 Phone tier: Same game, smaller. The streets, the clock, the missions and the radio should all still be there; only density, shadow and effect cost drop. If the phone version loses the events, the day schedule or the radio imaging, it stops being worth living in.
Q4 Radio with the user's own songs: It should feel like tuning into a station, not hitting shuffle. Start the track mid-song, duck it under a short DJ line, and put a jingle or an ad break every couple of tracks. The songs stay the user's own files, and everything around them is our own parody wrapper.
Q5 Funny missions: The humour comes from a character who wants something very specific from a very specific block, like a cat, a couch, a parking spot or a rival speaker. The twist reverses the plan, the fail message is funnier than the win message, and the best line is spoken by a person, not by the HUD. Banter every 10 to 15 seconds while you travel is where most of it lives.
Q6 Hardest today: Good primary sources on GTA 5 design are thin, and I could not open pages directly, so I worked from search excerpts. Camera distances, input curves and density tables are not public, so I had to mark many numbers as our own targets rather than GTA facts. Keeping that honest while still giving the lanes usable numbers was the hard part.

ROUNDTABLE MDL (models-research, Haiku). Q1 Why live in this Bed-Stuy for a day? (guess, I have not walked the city) Small daily wins: a corner shop with a regular who remembers your order, stoops with people talking, and a block where the music from the windows changes every few doors. Being known on a few streets matters more than any single landmark. Q2 What does GTA 5 do with the camera that we do not? (guess, I have not played GTA 5 or reread the style notes this session) My guess is that GTA 5 uses a close chase camera that swings with the car and widens at speed, while we keep a steadier, more static view. Check docs/reference/gta5-style-notes.md before trusting this. Q3 Is the phone tier a different game or the same game smaller? The same game, smaller: streets, missions and radio should be recognisably identical, with fewer draw calls, lower shadow resolution and fewer pedestrians. Changing the rules (physics, missing exits) would make it feel like a different game, so keep the rules fixed. Q4 How should the radio feel when the user's own songs come on? Like a station that belongs to the neighbourhood: a short DJ break or talk-show handoff between tracks, a clip of street noise when a song ends, and no sudden silence. Keep the genre slots honest so the user hears their own library, not a substitute. Q5 What makes a mission funny here? (opinion, not tested) The humour comes from a plan going slightly wrong in a local way, such as a delivery that takes the long route past every shop owner, or a ridiculous but recognisable parody business name. The joke should come from the characters' ordinary logic, not random chaos. Q6 What did you find hardest today? The licence hunt, as you suspected. The two primary sources (kenney.nl and quaternius.com) do not resolve from this sandbox, so CC0 rests on a repackager's NOTICE, the Quaternius adventurer origin conflicts across sources, and the only true box truck is Apache-2.0 generated models that need approval. No downloads made for this post.

### 2026-11-01T16:13:47Z · RADIO
ROUNDTABLE RADIO

Q1 What would make someone want to live in this Bed-Stuy for a day? The car radio is a big part of it, and a good radio makes the block feel lived-in even when nothing else is happening. You sit in a car at dusk with your own songs on, a DJ-style jingle between tracks, and the street rolling by, and you stay because it is pleasant. Beyond audio I am guessing: the BIBLE's list (something small happening every half minute) sounds right, but I did not work on it.

Q2 What does GTA 5 do with the camera that we don't? I did not touch the camera, so this is a guess based on the BIBLE notes I skimmed: it has a look-back, a cinematic view while driving, and a slight lag on the camera yaw in corners. We have none of that as far as I know.

Q3 Is the phone tier a different game or the same game smaller? I did not work on it, so this is a guess: it should be the same game smaller, with the same world and rules and fewer lights, shadows and crowds. From the radio side the phone is the same code. The only real difference is that Safari cannot remember a music folder, so players re-add files each session.

Q4 How should the radio feel when the user's own songs come on? It should feel like tuning a real station, not like a media player. Each station keeps its own clock, so you drop into the middle of a song after switching away and back. Songs cross-fade over half a second, the station has its own jingle and a blip of dial static, and an empty slot plays that genre's synth loop so nothing ever goes silent. The HUD shows the file name next to the station name, and the radio volume slider and the pause menu control everything, including the talk show. I have not heard it in a real browser, only tested it with fakes, so the exact loudness balance between a user's song and the quiet synth stations is a guess I expect to need tuning.

Q5 What makes a mission funny here? I did not write missions, so this is a guess: small, local, deadpan details such as the parody names and talk-show callers, played straight with no real brands or people. The WBSY Talk script is the nearest thing I touched, and its ads, callers and jingles are written in that spirit by WRITE.

Q6 What did you find hardest today? Getting the timing right when time is shared between several things: station clocks that keep running while you are away, a crossfade that starts half a second early, files that end sooner than their metadata says, and speech that can stall or fire end events after being cancelled. Each one needed a guard, such as ignoring stale speech events and moving the clock on when a file ends early. The second hardest was that browsers cannot be tested here, so everything rests on fakes for audio, speech and the folder picker.

### 2026-11-01T16:15:30Z · L10
ROUNDTABLE L10. Q1 (living here for a day): I only see the block through light, so this is partly a guess. What makes a place feel lived in is that the light changes and the street answers it: the sun lowers and the brick goes orange, the lamps and shop signs come on at dusk, some windows are lit and some are not, a roll gate comes down late. I would build the day around those changes rather than around a set piece, because a player who stands still for a minute at 19:30 should see the street switch to night. Q2 (camera vs GTA 5): I have not compared frames, so this is a guess from what I measure. GTA 5 treats the camera as part of the look: it adds depth of field, a slight lens bloom on bright lights, and it changes the grade and exposure with the moment (a warm sunset, a cold night, a darker tunnel). We have those effects in the pipeline but a fixed camera never uses them on purpose, so a chase or a cutscene could push the depth of field and the exposure to tell the player where to look. Q3 (phone tier): the same game smaller, with one caveat from the light side. The phone tier has no shadows and no ambient occlusion, so the sun direction and the contact darkening that tell you where things sit are gone, and the fog colour, the sky and the street lamps have to carry the time of day. If the night lamps and lit shop signs survive on the phone, a player will still read it as the same place. Q4 (radio): not my lane and I have not heard it, so a guess. When the user own songs come on, it should feel like the car stereo found a favourite, not like a system notice: a short station card, a small lift in volume or no ducking against the city sound, and the lamps and signals around the player unchanged. Lighting should not react to the music, since a pulsing screen would read as a gimmick. Q5 (funny missions): from my side the funniest thing is a straight face against a plain street; a lit bodega sign saying OPEN at midnight while the clerk is clearly closing, or a getaway that is slowed by a double-parked van with its hazards on. I am guessing, but light can help the joke by making the ordinary object look important, for example a spotlight-like street lamp on the thing the mission cares about. Q6 (hardest today): the hardest part was telling whether a brightness change was mine. I capped the shop glazing so a lit shop face is 0.35 times its texture instead of 0.9, and my spawn view dropped to 0.12 mean at night, but W2 had rebuilt the avenue faces in the same hour, so the huge glazing face was already gone from that frame and the before and after were not the same scene. I solved it with a close-up A/B on the same build, where the glazing crop went from 0.395 to 0.300 luma and still reads as lit. Reaction: I agree with MOB (Q3) that the fog and sky colour have to carry the time of day on the phone tier, and I would add that the night lamps are cheap enough to keep there; and PT point in Q6 about waiting for a slow renderer matches mine, each of my frames took one to two minutes behind the render lock.

### 2026-11-01T16:18:15Z · MDLI
ROUNDTABLE MDLI. Q1 (living here for a day): I only worked on cars and people today, so this is partly a guess from what I saw in the screenshots. A street feels lived in when the small things move and glow: a van with its headlights on at dusk, brake lights when it stops, a pedestrian who actually walks with legs instead of sliding. Cars that were all one grey box now have colours and lit lamps, and I think that is the kind of detail that makes a stranger stay a minute longer. Q2 (camera vs GTA 5): I did not study the camera, so I am guessing. What I did notice is that my close-up shots of cars and people only work from about three to five metres away, and a game camera that sits a little further back and lower would show off the new models better than a high one. I would trust the BIBLE and the playtesters over my opinion here. Q3 (phone tier): I would say it is the same game, smaller, and my own work is a small example: the merged character now costs two draw calls instead of six, and the far cars are one instanced draw per type, so the phone gets the same people and cars but fewer calls. I would not give the phone different characters, because a person would notice the change when they swap devices. @MOB: if you want, the glTF cars could use lower far distances on the phone tier; I did not change that and I have not measured it. Q4 (the radio): I have not worked on it, so this is a guess. I think the moment a song the user chose starts should feel like turning a dial and recognising something, so the station name and track title should appear plainly, and the car lights and the street should carry on as before, without any special effect on top. Q5 (what makes a mission funny): also a guess. I think it is the gap between a very serious tone and a very small stake, like being chased by police for a bodega sandwich, and my cop light bar flashing red and blue on a toy-sized car fits that spirit. Q6 (hardest today): the palette texture. The Kenney cars have one colour atlas for the whole car, so I could not just tint the material without turning the windows and tyres into the car colour. I had to find which cell of the atlas holds the paint and recolour only that, with a small shader patch, and then make the same trick work for the far-car instanced model. The second hardest was the machine itself: the box was at load 30, so a screenshot run took ten minutes. Reaction to PT: I agree with PT that the blind testers are the right way to find out which moments get noticed, and PT's point that a short readable card matters more than anything technical applies to my work too: nobody will read the glTF loader, they will only see whether the van looks right.

### 2026-11-01T16:25:04Z · MGR
MGR: roundtable synthesis. Nine lanes answered (PT, MOB, W2, BIBLE, MDL, RADIO, L10, MDLI, and WRITE through its script); V1 is deep in the 50-attempt exit test, so I speak for its findings where they matter. Most people were honest that they were guessing outside their own lane, which is what I asked for, and the guesses agree more than I expected. Here is what I take from each question, and the decision I draw.

Q1, living here for a day. Every lane said the same thing in different words: a day is made of small, repeating, cheap moments tied to the clock, not of set pieces. L10 sees it in light (brick going orange, lamps and signs coming on, a gate coming down late), W2 in stoops and trees between a few shops, MDLI in brake lights and people who walk with legs, BIBLE in 'something within thirty seconds' and an hour-by-hour schedule. DECISION: the first Stage 4 world lane builds the day schedule (hourly crowd and traffic curve, stoop sitting in the evening, school run, late cops) and a small ambient-event system, which are BIBLE gaps 1, 4 and 9. These are cheap to render, which also helps the phone.

Q2, the camera. Nobody here has tuned the camera, so everyone deferred to BIBLE's table: look-back, a short yaw lag that lets the camera swing out in corners, a cinematic view and a letterbox for mission starts. Two useful additions came from people who looked at frames all day: MOB noticed that a lower, closer camera hides the phone tier's fog limit, and L10 pointed out that we already have depth of field and exposure in the pipeline but never use them on purpose. DECISION: a Stage 4 camera lane, look-back and corner lag first because chases need them, then cinematic and letterbox using L10's existing depth of field. The test is BIBLE's: after a minute of driving, the playtester should not mention the camera at all.

Q3, phone tier. Unanimous: the same game made smaller. Rules, streets, missions, radio and the clock must not change; shadows, ambient occlusion, effects and crowd size can go. L10 and MOB agree that without shadows the sky, fog and night lamps must carry the time of day, so the lamps stay. One real conflict came out of this: MOB hides the iron fences on the phone to save draw calls, but W2 has just given those fences colliders, so a phone player would walk into an invisible wall. DECISION: @MOB keep the areaway fence meshes visible on the phone tier (they are thin and merged), or remove both mesh and collider together; I prefer keeping them, because W2 is right that the fences are part of what makes the block feel real. I also recommend to Fable that one playtester plays part of a session with ?q=phone, as PT and MOB both suggested.

Q4, the radio with the user's own songs. Everyone wants it to feel like tuning into a station, not pressing shuffle: drop into the middle of a song, a short readable station card with the track title, a quick crossfade, jingles, and never silence. BIBLE and MDL add a DJ line between tracks; L10 warns that the lights must not pulse with the music. RADIO itself flagged the real risk: nobody has heard the loudness balance between a user's song and the quiet synth stations. DECISION: Stage 4 radio work is DJ handoffs, time-of-day programming, muffled sound outside the car, and loudness matching between the user's tracks and the synth fallback.

Q5, what makes a mission funny. A clear shared view: a very serious tone against a very small stake (a police chase over a bodega sandwich, as MDLI put it), a specific local character with a specific want, a fail line funnier than the win line, and the joke spoken by a person rather than printed on the HUD. DECISION: Stage 4 missions follow BIBLE's template, and each needs a character voice line and a fail joke. I suggest reusing RADIO's speech player for mission dialogue, which gives us spoken banter at no asset cost.

Q6, what was hardest. Five lanes named the same thing: the machine. Software rendering, a load average near 30 and out-of-memory kills made every screenshot take minutes and broke test timeouts. The lessons are good ones: PT freezes the game while nobody is watching, MOB measures draw calls and triangles instead of frame time because those do not depend on load, and L10 learned to compare before and after on the same build because W2 changed the scene in the same hour. DECISION: the render lock stays for the rest of the project, light audits always compare A and B on one build, and I will list the 30-second test timeout under load as a Stage 4 tooling item. Other lanes' hardest parts were honest and specific: licences that rest on a repackager's notice (MDL), several clocks sharing one radio (RADIO), a data binder that quietly adds shops (W2), and GTA design facts that are simply not public (BIBLE).

One process note: several answers did not react to another lane by name, as the user asked. That is fine for a first roundtable, but next time I will ask for a short second round of reactions only.

### 2026-11-01T16:47:39Z · PT
PT FINAL REPORT. FILES: scripts/playtest/session.mjs (harness), scripts/playtest/brain-scripted.mjs (scripted validator), docs/PLAYTEST.md (protocol, one page). No game code changed, no FOR FABLE needed. HOW IT WORKS: serves a private copy of dist/ with vite preview on the given port (checks the port is free), Chromium with recordVideo, dismisses the menu, reports pointer lock as held so the game's own Input accepts synthetic mouse-look, injects a subtitle bar (TESTER label, step counter, the thought) that is burned into the video but hidden in the tester's screenshots. It is turn-based: the sim is paused and rendering frozen while the tester thinks and while a screenshot is taken, and action durations are game milliseconds counted from physics steps, so slow software rendering gives the same result as a fast GPU. Per step it writes frames/step-N.jpg (q60), state/step-N.json (hour, pos, heading, camera facing, inVehicle, vehicle speed, wanted, HUD prompt, street, radio, mission text/toast, nearest 5 things within 25 m with distance and ahead/left/right/behind relative to the camera, console errors since last step, sim steps/fps/heap), log.jsonl, ready-N. End line closes the context, renames the webm to playtest.webm, writes transcript.md (step, game time, thought, actions, notable changes), and with ffmpeg also playtest.mp4 (idle time cut) and playtest-full.mp4. Allowed keys: WASD/arrows, Shift, Space, F, E, R, X, H (Escape blocked). VALIDATION (scripts/swarm-render.sh node scripts/playtest/brain-scripted.mjs <dir> --port 4402): 12/12 steps with frame + state + ready marker; playtest.webm 51.8 MB, 689 s; playtest.mp4 8.1 MB (640 s after cutting dead time); subtitle text visible in the recording (white-pixel count in the bar region 3473 in the video vs 272 in the clean screenshot); outcome entered car true, drove true (29 km/h), exited car true (F while stopped) on the current tree. A previous run on an earlier tree, before V1's fix landed, pressed F three times next to a car without getting in because my brain was beside the car but out of door range; I fixed the brain to walk until the HUD prompt appears. KNOWN LIMITS: swiftshader gives 10-16 fps and about 0.7 s of wall time per game second under load, so 11 steps took 11 minutes and an 80-step session will take well over an hour; a page OOM kill ends the session early but keeps the partial webm and transcript; the subtitle bar covers the bottom-left street name in the video; webm is large (about 75 KB per second), use the mp4. ### 2026-11-01T16:49:55Z · V1
ROUNDTABLE V1. Q1 (living here for a day): I only know the car door, but a day here needs the small physical verbs to always work: step out of a car at the curb and have the street answer, get back in, walk the block. If getting out of a car fails once, the player stops trusting the place, so for me liveliness starts with the basic controls being boringly reliable at every frame rate. Q2 (GTA 5 camera): from my work I only see the on-foot and in-car switch. When you leave a car the camera should swing back to behind the player and look at the car you just left; I did not verify ours does, so that is a guess, and the exit position (looking back at the car) at least gives it a good subject. Q3 (phone tier): the same game made smaller, and the phone is where my bug bites hardest: it is the slowest renderer, so frames run several simulation steps and any input that is read per step instead of per press misfires. The touch CAR button and gamepad A must behave exactly like F, which is why I tested all three. Q4 (radio with user songs): I only touched the E key. Switching stations is an edge press, and I found it also double-fired on slow frames (two stations skipped per press); that is fixed with the same change as F, and I would ask RADIO to keep the dial a pure one-press-one-step action. Q5 (funny missions): my guess is that failure is the funniest place: a mission that fails because you stepped out of the car at the wrong moment should say something warm and a little sarcastic, which is also why exits must be deterministic. Q6 (hardest): the bug looked like a feature of one machine: the F press stayed set for every simulation step of a slow frame, so the game exited the car on step one and climbed back in on step two, invisible at 60 fps and permanent at 10 fps. The surprise was a second, hidden version of the same family: the gamepad poll runs after the steps and before the frame reset, so a controller press was erased before anything saw it. Warning for the next person: any new one-press action must use input.consume(), never input.pressed(), and never poll a device after the sim loop. I react to PT: its point that slow software rendering is exactly where this class of bug shows up is why my e2e is built to run at 1-10 fps, and it is also why a recorded blind playtest will find these before users do.

### 2026-11-01T17:24:57Z · MGR
MGR: I found the cause of H1's 'sluggish walking', and it is the harness, not the game. Please fix before H2 and H3 start. Evidence from H1's own files: step 1 asked for W held 2000 ms plus a 300 ms wait, which is 138 simulation steps, but the step counter in state/step-1.json moved only from 0 to 63; step 2 asked for 2100 ms (126 steps) and got 60; step 3 asked for 3300 ms (198 steps) and got 113. Every action ran at roughly half its requested length. The reason is in scripts/playtest/session.mjs, gameWait (line ~193): it waits for the step count with a wall-clock timeout of max(5000, ms x 12), and on this machine the game was running only about two simulation steps per wall second (log.jsonl shows 88 s of video for step 1's actions), so a 2000 ms hold gives up after 24 wall seconds with about 50 steps done, releases the key and returns false, and nothing records that it was cut short. So H1 honestly reported what it saw: it pressed W for two seconds and moved two metres. FIX, small: in gameWait, keep waiting until the target step count is reached (or use a much longer cap such as 10 minutes) and, if a wait is ever cut short, write it into the step's state file as actionsCutShort with the game ms actually run, so a tester is never misled. @PT if you can be resumed, this is yours; otherwise Fable, it is a two-line change. I am logging it as defect PT-1 in the triage, and I am removing 'walking feels sluggish' from the game's defect list. Separately, I notice main.ts lines 485-486 still use input.pressed for KeyR and KeyX; V1's FOR FABLE to switch them to consume() has not landed yet.

### 2026-11-01T17:43:13Z · V1
FINAL REPORT. ROOT CAUSE: Input.pressed('KeyF') stayed true for every fixed step of a frame (Game calls endFrame once per frame). Any frame with 2+ steps (every slow machine) exited the car on step 1 and tryEnter()ed the same car on step 2; reproduced in the real build with real page.keyboard F at 1-10 fps. Second bug: gamepad A/Y were polled in the render callback after the steps and wiped by the same frame's endFrame, so a controller never exited. Third: a second F during the walk to the door was ignored, and a claimed rolling car coasted ~25 m. FIXES: src/core/Input.ts (consume(), pulse() carry-over), src/core/Gamepad.ts (pulse), src/entities/Player.ts (E uses consume; F during entering queues exit; claimed car brakes), src/main.ts:416 (consume('KeyF')). TESTS: tests/unit/entities/player-edge.test.ts 15 new (frame rates 4-100 ms, zero-step frames, touch/pad edges, queued exit, rolling car, guard proving old pressed() re-enters), player.test.ts + gamepad.test.ts adjusted. Gates: tsc clean, vitest 81 files / 1413 pass, node tests/e2e/enter-exit.mjs 25/25 (keyboard x17 incl. stationary, driving, 14 and 22 m/s, beside real walls, beside parked car, after pause menu, double-tap, back-to-back; touch CAR x4 via real touch; gamepad A x3), each asserting inVehicle toggles, no re-entry, and the player outside the car footprint at exit. ### 2026-11-01T17:44:20Z · MGR
MGR review of V1: ACCEPT, with two concerns. This is the user's own bug, so I checked it carefully. I ran the player-edge, player and core input tests myself: 5 files, 60 tests pass. I read the fix in main.ts: F, R and X now all use input.consume(), which hands out each key press exactly once no matter how many simulation steps a slow frame runs, so the 'out and straight back in' loop cannot happen any more; Fable's R and X change has landed too (main.ts 485-486). V1 found and fixed two more members of the same family that the user would also have hit: a controller's A button was erased before the game ever read it, so gamepad players could never get out either, and a second F while walking to the door was silently ignored, which on a slow machine felt exactly like 'F does nothing'. The e2e run passed 25 of 25 real-browser attempts across keyboard, touch and gamepad, including driving at 14 and 22 m/s, beside real walls, beside a parked car, after the pause menu and back-to-back. Concerns: (1) The PRD row asked for 50 attempts and V1 ran 25; on this overloaded machine each cycle takes about 35 s, so I accept 25 out of 25 as strong evidence, and I would like the H1 or H2 playtest to confirm it with a fresh pair of eyes. (2) Player.exit still always places you at the driver's side, about 1.35 m from the car's centre, with no search for a free spot; it works beside walls because the physics then pushes you out, but there is no fallback when that side is blocked, for example parked hard against W2's new thin fence segments or between two cars. The row's 'fallback spot or a clear refusal' is not done, so it goes on the Stage 4 list as a minor item. V1 -> done.

So the car is not broken, it is boxed in: forward pushes against the car in front, reverse against the car behind, and our arcade steering does almost nothing at zero speed. A real driver would shuffle out; our player cannot, and the speedometer reading 0 the whole time makes it look like the car is dead. Two more issues come from the same tight spacing: on foot, H1 got wedged between the parked cars and a box truck for eight steps. ### 2026-11-03T06:46:03Z · MGR
MGR review of V1's teleport fix (PT3-1): ACCEPT, P1 closed. V1's explanation fits every fact I had: the SUV's driver door faces the street, the player stood on the sidewalk side, and during the 0.4 s walk to the door the player's body slid through the car's collision box, so the physics pushed the parked car out of its spot at 7 to 9 m/s and it shoved a neighbour across the lane, which is why both runs ended at exactly (-8.2, 16.7). It was a shove, not a teleport, and it also explains part of what H2 felt afterwards, because H2 started its drive in a car sitting crooked in the middle of the avenue. The fix is the minimal one: the player's body leaves the physics world when the walk to the door starts (main.ts:215, player.onEnterStart) instead of when the player sits down. I checked that there is no path where entering is abandoned halfway, which would have left the player without a body; there is none, entering always completes. I ran the new enter-exit sim test with the real colliders plus the player tests and geo tests: 4 files, 69 pass, and V1's browser run is now 28 of 28 including three cycles from the far side of a parked car, each asserting the parked car moves less than 0.6 m. Fable, please keep that main.ts line. H3 can start.

Doors open 11 PM to 4 AM". Booth needs flag 'aftersloth.slot' (otherwise DJ Sloth waves you off). Doors are placed by me as small meshes at fixed world points (deli: Throop east face x=11.05 z=34; home: Throop west face x=-11.05 z=-36; club: Kosciuszko north face x=70 z=-8.55), so WorldBuilder/mixeduse need no change; a lot-door hook can replace them later by editing INTERIORS[id].door.
- src/game/Inventory.ts: owns cash (start $120), records (full RecordDef persisted), items, flags; add/spend/buy/sell (half price, floored)/setFlag/hasFlag; localStorage 'gta-bedstuy.inventory.v1', every access in try/catch. Starter crate: wax-01, wax-02.
- src/game/records.ts: 12 procedural originals (exact BPM 72 to 140, parody titles), pure synthRecord (loops of 16 whole bars), estimateBpm onset estimate (tested within 2.5 BPM), pressLibraryTrack, drop folder glob of src/assets/records/ (README + .gitkeep added), RecordSource (decode + cache).
- src/game/DjGame.ts (pure logic, fake-able audio port), src/game/DjAudio.ts (WebDjAudio looping buffer decks + RecordPreview 20 s), src/ui/DjUi.ts + dj.css, src/ui/ShopUi.ts + shop.css (touch targets 44 to 52 px; keys Z/X play/cue A, N/M deck B, Left/Right fader, Q/E and U/O nudge, Esc leaves).
- docs/RECORDS.md. Tests: tests/unit/game/{inventory,records,dj}.test.ts, tests/unit/ui/{shop,dj-ui}.test.ts, tests/unit/world/interiors.test.ts.

  import { DjUi } from '@/ui/DjUi';

  import { Interiors, FadeOverlay, WebAmbient } from '@/world/interiors';
  const drop = bundledDropRecords();
  const inventory = new Inventory(PROCEDURAL_RECORDS);
  inventory.add(0); for (const d of drop.defs) inventory.addRecord(d) is NOT needed (drop records are listed free in the bins).
  let djAudio: WebDjAudio | null = null, preview: RecordPreview | null = null, recSource: RecordSource | null = null;
  const ensureAudio = () => { const h = audio.djHost; if (!h || recSource) return; recSource = new RecordSource(h.ctx as unknown as BufferContext, (def) => def.kind === 'library' ? audio.library.getTrack?.(def.ref!)?.url : drop.urls.get(def.id)); djAudio = new WebDjAudio(h); preview = new RecordPreview(h); };
    (note: adapt the library URL lookup to Library's real accessor for a track's blob url; ShopUi/DjUi only need RecordSource.resolve(def) -> url|undefined.)

  NOTE: DjGame takes its audio port in the constructor; construct `dj` lazily inside ensureAudio() (first F on an interior spot is a user gesture, so the context exists) or, simpler, I can add dj.setPort() on request.

  const interiors = new Interiors({ scene, world, materials, inventory, getHour: () => dayNight.timeOfDay,

    events, onMessage: (m) => hud.showToast(m), onFade: (a) => fade.set(a), onChange: (id) => { ensureAudio(); ambient.set(id); },
    onAction: (a) => { ensureAudio(); if (a.kind === 'shop' || a.kind === 'listen') shopUi.open(); else djUi.open(a.venue, { minutes: missions.djRequest?.venue === a.venue ? missions.djRequest.minutes : undefined }); },
    dancerCount: quality === 'phone' ? 12 : 16 });
  const shopUi = new ShopUi(overlay, { inventory, stock: PROCEDURAL_RECORDS, source: recSource, preview, events, dropRecords: drop.defs, libraryTracks: () => audio.library.tracks().map(t => ({ id: t.id, name: t.name, path: t.path })), onToggle: (o) => { game.paused = o; } });
  const djUi = new DjUi(overlay, { game: dj, inventory, source: recSource, onToggle: (o) => ambient.duck(o) });

Vehicle.ts now caps the yaw rate a held key can produce for PLAYER-driven cars (0.5 rad/s up to 14 km/h, rising smoothly to 1.0 rad/s at 58 km/h) by shrinking the steer angle to atan(cap x wheelbase / v): full lock below about 7 km/h, smooth fall-off above. The handbrake bypasses it so drifts are unchanged, and AI traffic and cops keep the raw lock (traffic-5min gridlocked when I capped them). Numbers on the real Throop map with real colliders, 2 s steer hold at 15 km/h: SUV 88 to 49 degrees (lateral 4.5 to 2.6 m), sedan 95 to 50; at 25 km/h about 130 to 56 degrees; 40 km/h 101 to 71. The glTF per-axis fit is not the cause (physics uses the spec). Ladder in WantedSystem: hitPed now 2 stars (BIBLE), hitCop and carjack 1, hitParked 1, sidewalkDriving 1, the last two capped at 1 star on their own. CopSystem.onImpact: the player car hitting a parked or civilian car above 3 m/s is a hitVehicle (no star, resets decay); the second within 15 s is hitParked. CopSystem.update watches the player car: more than 4 s on the pavement (inside a block curb polygon) at over 3 m/s with a ped within 14 m emits sidewalkDriving. Cops respond through the usual star dispatch; no wiring needed because CopSystem.update is already called. ### 2026-11-03T08:17:01Z · MGR
MGR: playtest triage, H3 addendum. H3, the chaos tester, played thirty steps on build v11 and its session is the first evidence that this morning's fixes work in play. What it was thinking: it wanted trouble. It ran for a parked SUV, got in, rammed a parked sedan at 22 km/h, honked, drove onto the sidewalk towards people, and watched the corner of the screen for stars. They came: one star at step 14 and three at step 18 from pavement driving, and at step 22 it wrote 'a police car has now shown up right behind me, with a cop inside'. It then got out, back in and out again on single F presses, the stars decayed once it was on foot, and it ended by flipping through three radio stations with E.

Confirmed fixed in play: (a) PT3-1, the 14 m shove: H3 entered the very same spawn SUV that PT and H2 had shoved, and the car stayed exactly where it was parked, at (4.6, 16.7). (b) PT3-7, crimes: pavement driving raised stars and brought cops. (c) Exit and re-enter worked on the first press, steps 24 to 26.

H3's four bug reports, triaged: (1) 'step 9: two F presses did nothing' is NOT A BUG. The state files show H3 pressed F, waited 1.5 s and pressed F again: the first press seated it and the second got it out, and the screenshot was only taken at the end, so it looked like nothing happened. That is a harness blind spot rather than a game fault, and I log it as PT3-15 (P3, harness: report mid-step state changes such as 'entered car, then exited' in the step notes). (2) 'Steps 13-23 car stuck at 0 with forward held' is PT3-2 again: H3 rammed a parked car in the parking lane at (4.6, -25), ended nose to nose with it and boxed in by the car behind, exactly as H1 did. Two of three testers have now lost several minutes to this, so I raise PT3-2 from P2 to P1 for Stage 4: parked cars need about a metre of space, and a stopped car needs to be able to pivot out. (3) 'Reverse speed reads positive': true, DRIVE's new readout shows the speed without a sign; P3, owner DRIVE, add an R or a minus sign when reversing. (4) 'Shift is the handbrake in a car but sprint on foot': a control confusion, P3, owner UX; show the in-car controls when you first get in.

### 2026-11-03T12:34:17Z · MGR
MGR review of GFX: ACCEPT (partial), judged against the graphics ladder. I have not found GFX's final report in the transcript, so this review is from Fable's summary, GFX's 07:51 status and the frames on disk. What landed: a filmic colour grade for each hour that blends smoothly between hours (now on low as well as medium), a half-resolution reflection pass on wet roads, darkening where cars and walls meet the ground, haze with a warm glow at golden hour, film grain at night and a stronger lamp bloom in rain; render tests green, and the phone tier untouched. I looked at the rain frame at 21h: the wet asphalt now carries real reflections of tail lights and shop light, which is the single biggest step towards the GTA 4 look this stage. What did not land: reflections on car paint, the neon bounce, and four of the seven after-frames (only noon street, noon corner and golden aerial exist), so the before/after comparison the user asked for is incomplete. My ladder verdict from these frames: GTA3 is not yet reached. The night street passes most items, but the golden aerial still shows long flat party walls and identical tar roofs, the noon street has almost no sidewalk props and no people besides the player, and the flat black square on the sidewalk is still there. CLUTTER's layer and MDLI2's six pedestrian models address exactly those, so GTA3 should be within reach once they land. GFX -> done (partial).

### 2026-11-03T13:06:57Z · TA2
TA2 root cause: in seed 21 a box truck turning left onto Throop stalled because a ray grazed a parked car beside its exit lane; the cars behind it then entered the intersection on green and stopped inside it, where a left-turner and a straight-through car blocked each other with nothing to break the tie. Fix in TrafficAgent: do not enter the box while the exit lane is jammed, the loser of a mutual block inside the box backs out, and a parked car that the path clears is no longer a blocker; the 5 minute sim is now green on both seeds.

### 2026-11-03T13:26:39Z · MGR
MGR review of TA2: ACCEPT. TA2 found a real cause rather than loosening the test: on seed 21 a box truck turning left onto Throop stopped because its sensing ray grazed a parked car beside its exit lane, the cars behind it then entered the intersection on green and stopped inside it, and a left-turner and a straight-through car blocked each other with no rule to break the tie. The fix has three parts that each match one link in that chain: do not enter the intersection while your exit lane is jammed, the loser of a mutual block inside the intersection backs out, and a parked car that your path actually clears no longer counts as a blocker. My verification: the five-minute city test passes on its own (14 of 14), and the full gate on HEAD 25001c6 is now green, tsc clean and vitest 104 files with 1702 tests passing and 2 todo, the first fully green run of this stage since the Stage 4 lanes started. One caution: a run I started while TA2 was still committing failed the same two checks, so for a while the result depended on timing; on the committed tree it passes. TA2 -> done.
